`forbidden-bundle-constants.json` records each slot VA, raw64-bit string words and backing-text VA. Most use Mach-O chained pointer encodings and Swift long strings; they are **not raw runtime pointers**. In this sample their resolved backing text spans0x100092fc0–0x1000931b0. Two are small Swift strings: com.apple.tccd at0x1000b0460 and com.apple.dock at0x1000b0490. The first word includes Swift representation flags; its low length bits specify the literal length. The extracted bytes were directly checked against mapped Mach-O sections. Use exact listed bytes, not similar current Apple app names.

### PID check algorithm

`check(pid:allowOverride:)`0x1000322bc:

    if allowOverride: return
    if pid absent: return
    app = NSRunningApplication(processIdentifier:pid)
    if app absent: return
    bundle = app.bundleIdentifier
    if bundle absent: return
    if bundle not in forbidden set: return
    throw CUError(
        code: "forbidden_target",
        message: "target app " + bundle + " is on the CUA blocklist for user-safety reasons",
        hint: <semantic description: explicit force_forbidden override is mentioned for genuinely required system-privacy workflows; long UI wording is paraphrased, not a byte-exact fixture>
    )

Shortcircuits0x1000322d8/2ec/30c/320; exact membership0x10003235c; CUError construction0x100032418. Error code literal0x100092e60; suffix0x100092e80; long hint0x100092ec0. Missing/unresolvable app is a pass for **this gate**, not proof that later action succeeds.

### Bundle check algorithm

`check(bundleId:allowOverride:)`0x10003249c:

    if allowOverride or bundle absent or bundle not in set: return
    throw CUError(
        code: "forbidden_target",
        message: "target bundle " + bundle + " is on the CUA blocklist for user-safety reasons",
        hint: "Pass `force_forbidden: true` to override."
    )

Shortcircuits0x1000324b0/4b8/4ec; CUError construction0x1000325ac; short hint0x100092f90. This variant does not resolve running applications; an exact forbidden bundle is rejected even if not running.

### Dispatcher integration and exact coverage

Only direct calls to these check functions in the inspected disassembly are dispatcher0x100076968 (PID) and0x1000769c0 (bundle). That establishes this source-level gate's reachable call sites, not the absence of OS/host/plugin policy elsewhere.

PID-gated commands:

    click, perform_secondary_action, drag,
    type, type_text, press, press_key, scroll, set_value

Bundle-gated commands:

    intent, open_url

Static sets proof in DISPATCH_INVENTORY.md; key data/control0x100076708–67f0. `force_forbidden` read through bool helper0x100076924; absent/null/noncoercible -> optional tag2, then low bit0=>false. Bool true or nonzero Int=>true; string "true" is not accepted by helper. Wrapper/public schemas may not expose this field; do not add it to MCP tools on the strength of native availability alone.

For the action set:

    actionPID = resolveActionPid(args)  // see WIRE_SCHEMA
    check(pid:actionPID, allowOverride:args.bool("force_forbidden") ?? false)

Proof0x10007695c–968. It does not resolve the AX element ref to discover Entry.pid before checking. Consequently a ref-only action may pass nil-PID preflight and later resolve an e-ref's cached owner in AXBridge. A supplied PID can also differ from ref owner. This is an original coverage limitation; use a stronger final-resolved-target gate in a safer clone and mark that as an intentional deviation, rather than falsely documenting original protection as comprehensive.

`select_text`, `launch_app`, `raise`, `remote_input`, all snapshot/capture/list commands and native administrative/PIP/Appshot commands are not members of these two dispatcher sets. In particular list_apps does not filter out forbidden apps through this gate; snapshotting/listing may still describe them. No original all-actions blockade should be inferred.

### Conformance examples (use synthetic identities, no system interaction)

- exact com.apple.SecurityAgent -> true; lowercased com.apple.securityagent -> false
- exact com.apple.dock -> true; com.apple.dock.extra -> false
- nil bundle -> false; unknown bundle -> false
- PID resolving to a listed bundle -> forbidden_target with `target app` message + long hint
- direct listed bundle -> forbidden_target with `target bundle` message + short hint
- unresolvable PID -> this check returns; later action may separately fail
- original override false/nil -> normal gate; true/nonzeroInt -> gate returns
- ref-only click: preflight nil PID, no ref-owner lookup at this stage
- select_text and remote_input do not enter this dispatcher gate

No static unknown remains in this exact set/predicate/error/dispatcher contract. Remaining acceptance testing concerns OS bundle identity, other security layers and target ref mismatch behavior, not missing policy constants.

## REMOTE LENS EXTRA CONTRACTS
证据文件：analysis/native-snapshot/REMOTE_LENS_EXTRA_CONTRACTS.md


Static arm64 analysis of Alma 0.4.164. All addresses are unslid VAs in the same AlmaComputerUse sample as DISPATCH_INVENTORY.md. No target or replica was run. The contracts below are dispatcher-complete for parsed fields, ordinary defaults, success shapes and dispatcher-authored validation errors. Underlying CGEvent allocation, OS delivery, focus, permission and multi-display behavior remain separate runtime concerns.

### 1. Shared native readers: do not substitute strict JSON schemas

The optional dictionary readers first handle absent args, missing key, JSON null, and failed dynamic casts as nil. A nil optional then follows each command's own fallback/error behavior. Direct socket compatibility therefore differs from a stricter MCP schema.

| Reader | Function | Accepted conversion order and boundary |
|---|---|---|
| str | 0x100006140 | Dynamic cast to Swift String (cast at 0x100006200). No numeric-to-string conversion. Empty string is a valid optional String |
| int | 0x100006268 | Int cast (0x100006328), else Double cast (0x1000063ac), finite/range-checked truncation toward zero via fcvtzs (0x1000063bc–6400), else decimal String parse (0x100006404 onward, fallback parser at 0x1000066f0). Nonconvertible values return nil. Out-of-range/nonfinite Double conversion reaches brk instructions 0x1000066c8/cc/d0 instead of CUError |
| double | 0x10000671c | Double cast (0x1000067dc), else Int cast and numeric conversion (0x10000686c–6888), else String cast and complete locale-independent floating parse (0x1000068b4–6994). Failed conversion returns nil |
| bool | 0x1000069a8 | Bool cast (0x100006a68), else Int cast with zero=false and nonzero=true (0x100006af0–6b00). No string-bool parser is present |
| requireStr | 0x100087a50 | Calls str (0x100087a7c), then rejects both nil and empty string (0x100087a80–7a94). Error factory invalidArg at 0x100087ae8, exact message `missing "<key>"` with straight double quotes |

Dynamic-cast behavior above is established for values produced by this helper's AnyCodable decoder; cross-Foundation NSNumber bridging edge cases should be tested rather than generalized. Whitespace-only strings pass requireStr because it checks length, not trimming. Unknown extra dictionary keys are not read by these handlers.

Numeric values are not always safely rejected: a later checked Int32/UInt32 conversion can trap the process. A safer replica may turn these into validation errors, but that is a documented behavior change, not byte-compatible error handling.

### 2. remote_input complete dispatcher schema

Entry 0x10007dc60. This command routes desktop/display input, separately from app-targeted click/press_key/type_text. The command does not parse bundle, pid, window_id, ref, show_cursor, coord_space, or include_screenshot.

#### Common fields

| Field | Reader / required | Default or validation | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| kind | requireStr, required | Exact `pointer`, `scroll`, `key`, `text`; empty/missing/type mismatch: `missing "kind"` | 0x10007dc64–dc74; comparisons 0x10007df00/e2b4/e6d0/ebe4 |
| display_id | int, optional | nil uses CGMainDisplayID; parsed value checked to UInt32, negative/>4294967295 traps. Calls CGDisplayBounds for selected ID; no explicit active-display validation in this function | read 0x10007decc; RemoteControl.display 0x100024a14–4a70 |

Display resolution occurs **before** kind subdispatch, even for key/text and unknown kind. Thus malformed out-of-range display IDs can trap before a kind-validation error.

Unknown nonempty kind produces invalidArg with exact message `unknown remote_input kind "<kind>"` (prefix text VA0x100094c80, assembly0x10007f200–f284).

#### kind=pointer

| Field | Reader | Default / rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| x, y | double, required | Missing or nonconvertible either yields invalidArg: `remote_input pointer requires x and y (0…1 fractions)` | reads0x10007df1c/df3c; error0x10007df64, bytes0x100094ce0 |
| action | str, optional | nil -> `click`; empty string is not nil and errors. Exact enum values: `move`, `down`, `up`, `click`, `double`, `right`, `middle` | parse/default0x10007e368–e384; enum0x100024b4c→0x100026640; rawValue getter0x100024b50–4bfc |
| button | str, optional | nil -> `left`; valid `left`,`right`,`middle`. **Any unknown string also becomes left**, including empty; no invalidArg at this point | 0x10007e7b0–e7d8, MouseButton raw-value parse0x10001b1f0 |

Unknown pointer action error is invalidArg `unknown pointer action "<action>"` (0x10007e3d0–e42c, prefix bytes0x100094d20). Do not silently coerce an invalid action merely because invalid button is coerced.

Coordinates are fractions of the chosen display's CGDisplayBounds. RemoteControl.point at0x100024a74–4b48 clamps finite x/y independently into[0,1], then computes:

    screenX = CGRectGetMinX(bounds) + clamp(x,0,1) * CGRectGetWidth(bounds)
    screenY = CGRectGetMinY(bounds) + clamp(y,0,1) * CGRectGetHeight(bounds)

This uses display origins and full width/height, not width−1/height−1, not screenshot scaling, not a y-flip. NaN/infinity spellings accepted by low-level floating conversion were not run; do not infer finite-validation guarantees from the clamp.

Endpoint: RemoteControl.pointer(action:at:button:) at0x100024eb8, called0x10007e7f8. `right`/`middle` are distinct action enum values in addition to the button field. Persistent down/up/move behavior belongs to that endpoint and must not be rewritten as independent click calls.

#### kind=scroll

| Field | Reader | Default / rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| x, y | double, required | Same display-fraction clamp; error exact `remote_input scroll requires x and y (0…1 fractions)` |0x10007e2d0/e2f0; invalidArg0x10007e318, bytes0x100094ca0 |
| dx, dy | double, optional | Each independently defaults0 |0x10007e738/e75c; selects0 at0x10007e748–e76c |

Endpoint0x100025b14, called0x10007e780. It rounds each delta to nearest integer with ties away from zero (`frinta` at0x100025bcc and0x100025c08), checks Int32 representability, then passes dy as first wheel and dx as second wheel to CGEventCreateScrollWheelEvent2 (unit raw0, wheelCount2;0x100025c40–5c58). Nonfinite or out-of-range rounded values reach trap instructions0x100025db0–5dc4. It sets the event location, integer field0x58=1, synthetic sentinel field0x2a, then CGEventPost tap raw0. The OS meaning and delivery must be tested; no app PID is supplied in this route.

#### kind=key and kind=text

- `kind=key` requires nonempty String `key` (0x10007e6e0–e6f4). Calls RemoteControl.key at0x100025dc8 (call0x10007e708), which uses KeyParser.parse at0x100025de0 and emits a global down/up pair with parsed flags and synthetic sentinel. Key-parser errors propagate; its exact key grammar is covered by the native key contract.
- `kind=text` requires nonempty String `text` (0x10007ebf4–ec08). Calls RemoteControl.text at0x100025fd4 (call0x10007ec1c). It first tries KeyboardLayoutTranslator.keyPresses; on translation failure it falls back to Unicode scalar chunks. It does not share the `type_text` command's ref/input_method/verification result contract.
- x/y/action/button/dx/dy are not read by these two kind branches. display_id still influences the returned display metadata.

#### Success result and errors

All four kinds converge on **the same data object**, before the transport wraps it:

    { "ok": true, "display": { "id": <UInt32 as integer>, "width": <Int>, "height": <Int> } }

Construction0x10007ec30–ede8. `id` comes from resolved display0x10007eccc; width/height come from CGRect width/height, truncated toward zero with checked Int conversion0x10007ed0c–edb4. Dimensions describe CG display bounds; they are not screenshot pixel dimensions and do not include bounds origin.

The inner `ok:true` is **in addition to** the ordinary socket response envelope's `ok`. There is no screenshot, action echo, coordinate echo, pressed state or `delivered` field in this command's success data.

Underlying CGEvent errors propagate. Examples directly verified in endpoint code include internalError `CGEventSource creation failed`, `CGEvent creation failed (move)`, `CGEvent creation failed (scroll)`, `CGEvent creation failed (key)` (factories0x1000267b0,0x100025ccc/5d2c,0x100025ef0/5f54). Success means construction/posting returned without a thrown error, not that an application acknowledged the input.

### 3. lens complete dispatcher schema

Entry0x10007beec. Both fields use optional bool reader semantics from§1:

| Field | Missing/nonconvertible | Supplied parsed value | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| enabled | Leave current state unchanged | LensOverlay.setEnabled(value), called0x10007bf34 | parse0x10007bf08; optional sentinel check0x10007bf10–14 |
| fog | Leave current state unchanged | Call LensOverlay.setFogEnabled(value) | parse0x10007bf5c; call0x10007bf88 |

**In this build the fog setter is a no-op**: its symbol aliases the shared function body0x100009a9c, which consists of `ret`. `isFogEnabled` resolves to shared false-return body0x10000a7b4 (call0x10007c004). Therefore result `fog` is always false in this inspected binary, even after `{"fog":true}`. Do not implement a visible fog effect and label it observed compatibility.

Success data is always:

    { "enabled": <current Bool>, "fog": false }

Dictionary assembly0x10007bfc0–c020. No success inner `ok` key, screenshot or settings persistence is added in this path. No dispatcher-authored errors are present. With no args it is a status query. Singleton initial enabled=false, but CLI --lens can set it true before serving.

`setEnabled(false)` hides desktop Lens immediately; it does not by itself clear ActionCursor or disable other input. Native overlay lifecycle, independent ActionCursor state and native-vs-Electron cursor mapping are documented in `analysis/mirror-ui/native-cursor-contract.md`.

### 4. Scope and coverage ledger

Closed statically: remote_input/lens field spellings, native reader behavior, default selection, branch enum, dispatcher errors, data shapes, remote display selection and finite-coordinate mapping. No runtime verification performed.

Remaining endpoint work: full seven-action RemoteControl.pointer transition sequencing, all text fallback timing/chunk edge cases, OS/TCC delivery, hardware pointer/focus effects, and cross-client persistent drag state. Reuse the native input specialist's endpoint supplement when available; do not treat this dispatcher-level document as proof of background/no-focus safety.

Native MCPServer HTTP/auth/whitelist semantics and open_url/intent app-command mapping are not covered by this file. See their own contract status before claiming all45cmd implementation parity.

## PIP EXTRA CONTRACTS
证据文件：analysis/native-snapshot/PIP_EXTRA_CONTRACTS.md


### Scope and closure

**Closed by inspected instructions:** all five command argument keys, coercion helpers, selector precedence, defaults, top-level response shapes, nested session/armed status fields, custom PiP error messages, checked-integer traps, duplicate behavior, and minimization callback success/failure disposition. These are native daemon commands, not Electron auto-mirror IPC and not public MCP tool schemas.

**Partial only at the framework/runtime boundary:** actual ScreenCaptureKit/AX permission, protected-content, minimized-window, AppKit geometry enforcement and async race behavior cannot be proven by a static binary. Framework-generated localized descriptions remain dynamic. These limits do not leave the JSON schema unresolved.

No Alma code was executed. Evidence is static `analysis/native/arm64-disasm.txt`, `arm64-symbols.txt`, `arm64.macho`, and caller dispatch. Helper excerpts created in this directory are listed below. Virtual addresses are full arm64 addresses; the Mach-O slice __TEXT file offset is VA minus `0x100000000`. Static literal bytes were checked from the Mach-O, rather than trusting stale disassembler annotations.

See `WIRE_SCHEMA.md` for the outer newline JSON protocol: a successful command's result below is wrapped in `{id,ok:true,data:...}`; a CUError is wrapped in `{id,ok:false,error:{code,message[,hint]}}`. Object key order is not a compatibility guarantee.

### Shared input interpretation

All argument keys below live inside `args`.

- `str(key)` at `0x100006140`: String cast only; absent/null/wrong type gives nil. Empty String remains present.
- `int(key)` at `0x100006268`: Int, then Double converted to Int by truncation toward zero, then signed decimal String parse. Invalid String gives nil. Nonfinite/out-of-Int-range Double conversion traps rather than producing CUError. See `WIRE_SCHEMA.md` for JSON AnyCodable decoding and bridge nuances.
- `double(key)` at `0x10000671c`: Double, Int converted to Double, or numeric String; invalid/missing gives nil.
- `bool(key)` at `0x1000069a8`: Bool, then Int != 0; no String `"true"`/`"false"` parser. Optional nil is false for the `all` tests here.
- Whenever present, `window_id` is checked-converted to UInt32. Negative or >4294967295 causes a `brk #1` runtime trap, not a clean invalid_arg error. Zero is not rejected by dispatch.
- Whenever parsed, `pid` is checked-converted to Int32. Values outside -2147483648...2147483647 trap. The PIP dispatch resolver does not require positive PID on the direct-ID path.
- Unsupported extra argument keys are ignored. In particular there is no `ref`, `bundle_id`, `display_id`, `size`, `x`, `y`, `show_cursor`, or timeout field parsed by these command branches.

### 1. `pip_open`

Entry `0x10007c42c`; resolver `0x100088f74`; manager `0x1000485e0`.

#### Parsed arguments

| Key | Parser | Default / role |
|---|---|---|
| `window_id` | int then checked UInt32 | nil; selects direct-ID path if present |
| `pid` | int then checked Int32 | nil; optional metadata on direct-ID path, otherwise app selector |
| `bundle` | str | nil; read only on no-window_id app-resolution path |
| `title` | str | nil; metadata on direct-ID path, preferred title on app-resolution path |
| `width` | double | nil |
| `height` | double | nil; not even parsed if width did not parse |

#### Window resolution

Equivalent branch logic:

    if window_id parses:
        checked UInt32(window_id)
        pid = optional checked Int32(args.int("pid"))
        resolved = (window_id, pid, args.str("title") ?? "")
        // No resolveApp call and no ownership cross-check here.
        // bundle is ignored.
    else:
        app = resolveApp(bundle: args.str("bundle"),
                         pid: optional checked Int32(args.int("pid")))
        windows = (try? AXBridge.windows(app)) ?? []
        if windows.empty:
            throw internal("App has no windows to mirror.")
        selected = selectWindow(windows, args.str("title"))
        resolved = (selected.id, app.processIdentifier, selected.title)

`selectWindow`, in enumeration order:

1. If requested title is nonempty, return the first exact title equality match
2. Otherwise, still for a nonempty requested title, return the first whose title `contains(requestedTitle)` using Swift/Foundation String.contains without case-folding options
3. If title is absent/empty **or neither title match exists**, choose first `focused == true`
4. Else choose first window

A mismatching title is a preference, not an error. The fallback flag is definitely **focused**, not minimized or main: WindowInfo getter `0x1000109bc` reads byte +0x41; this is the flag tested at `0x100089244–248`. No minimized filter is present in this selection.

`resolveApp` is the shared AXBridge helper (`0x10001024c` wrapper, actual `0x100019718`): live explicit PID first; if unavailable, `bundle` exact bundle ID first match; if none, exact localized application name first match. With neither usable selector, error label is `pid=0`, not a frontmost-app default. See `WIRE_SCHEMA.md` for its app-not-found error.

Resolution evidence: direct-ID path `0x100088fa4–0x100089060`; app path `0x100089080–0x1000890d4`; exact/contains `0x1000890dc–0x10008921c`; focused/first `0x100089220–0x100089298`; no-windows error `0x10008929c–0x1000892fc`. AX window enumeration errors are caught and replaced with an empty array at `0x1000890b8–0x1000890cc`, thereby losing the underlying AX error.

#### Preferred size and title

Only if width **and** height parse and each compares >0 is `CGSize(width,height)` passed as the preferred size. Otherwise the entire preferred size is nil; a valid one-dimensional value is not used by itself. This is silent defaulting, not invalid_arg. Width is tested at `0x10007c980–984`, height at `0x10007c98c–994`, with nil tagging `0x10007c694–6a0`/`0x10007c970–99c`. NaN fails these comparisons; positive infinity is not explicitly rejected by dispatch.

For a new session with nil preferred size (`0x10004ad14–0x10004ada4`):

    sourceW = max(SCWindow.frame.width, 1)
    sourceH = max(SCWindow.frame.height, 1)
    aspect = sourceW / sourceH
    width = min(max(sourceW / 3, 240), 960)
    height = width / aspect

A provided positive pair replaces both calculated dimensions without aspect-ratio normalization. Constructor also sets the floater minimum size to 200×150 at `0x10004aec0–0x10004aed8`; actual enforcement by AppKit is a runtime boundary. The returned frame is the actual native window frame, so do not claim it always equals the requested content size.

An empty resolved title is changed to nil before manager.open (`0x10007c6a8–0x10007c7c0`). New-session title is `provided nonempty title ?? SCWindow.title ?? ""`, proven in both main-thread path `0x1000489f4–0x100048e48` and queued construction `0x100050b34–0x100050b7c`. `Session.title` stays the raw title; window chrome is separately labeled `Alma PIP` or `Alma PIP — <title>`.

#### Response

    {
      "opened": true,
      "session": {
        "window_id": 123,
        "pid": 456,
        "title": "Window title",
        "has_first_frame": false,
        "last_frame_age_ms": null,
        "floater_frame": {"x": 0.0, "y": 0.0, "w": 640.0, "h": 360.0}
      }
    }

Illustrative values only. Exact fields/types are defined under status below. A direct-ID open without pid produces `session.pid:null`; manager does not derive PID from SCWindow's owning application.

Existing session keyed by window_id: queue `bringToFront`, return its existing status. Do not replace session title/PID/size or report opened=false. The dispatcher always sets `opened:true` after successful manager.open (`0x10007c864–0x10007c8c4`). Existing check occurs at manager entry `0x1000486dc–0x100048714`, before fresh SCWindow lookup; status return `0x1000488b4–0x1000488c0`.

For a new session, SCWindow is looked up by ID through ShareableContentCache and the session is constructed on the main thread. Capture startup uses an async completion handler; a successful open response does **not** promise that a first frame has arrived. Off-main creation blocks via semaphore with an unbounded wait at `0x100048c70`; no request timeout field is used here.

#### Errors

- App-resolution errors from shared `resolveApp`
- `internal`: `App has no windows to mirror.`
- `internal`: `No SCWindow with id <UInt32> (window closed or off-screen?)` from `0x100049534–0x100049618`
- ShareableContentCache / ScreenCaptureKit / session-construction errors can propagate. Queued construction preserves a CUError; a non-CUError is wrapped as `internal(error.localizedDescription)` at `0x100048d08–0x100048df4`. The exact OS-localized description is not a fixed Alma string
- Checked-number traps described above

There is no error merely because the requested title did not match or width/height were absent/invalid/nonpositive.

### 2. `pip_close`

Entry `0x10007c70c`; manager single close `0x100049774`; close-all `0x100049d14`.

| Key | Parser | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `all` | bool | absent/invalid/false means single-window mode; true/nonzero Int means all |
| `window_id` | int then checked UInt32 | required only when `all` is false |

`all` is read first. If true, window_id is ignored, including invalid or out-of-range input. Close all then return:

    {"closed":"all"}

Otherwise missing/unparseable window_id throws `invalid_arg`, exact message:

    missing "window_id" (or pass "all": true)

For parsed UInt32 return:

    {"closed":true,"window_id":123}

`closed` is false if there was no matching session; that is a successful response, not an error. Bool means whether manager removed a session. Shutdown is queued after removal; reply is not proof every async stream shutdown operation has finished.

All branch and string result `0x10007c70c–0x10007c7b4`; ID/missing branch `0x10007c8e8–0x10007c96c`; response `0x10007cac0–0x10007cb80`. This command does not disarm minimize observers.

### 3. `pip_status`

Entry `0x10007c9e8`. No args are read; there is no single-ID selector.

    {
      "sessions": [SessionStatus, ...],
      "armed": [ArmedStatus, ...]
    }

Both arrays are always present, empty when none. Native code maps dictionary values/entries under each manager's own NSLock; it does not sort the results. The two lists are fetched separately (`0x10007ca3c`, `0x10007ca74`), so the combined result is not one atomic cross-manager snapshot.

#### SessionStatus (six exact fields)

Implementation `PIPSession.statusSnapshot`, `0x1000490a4–0x1000493e8`:

| Field | JSON type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `window_id` | integer | Session's UInt32 source window ID converted to Int |
| `pid` | integer or null | Session's optional Int32, sign-extended if present |
| `title` | string | Stored title, including empty String if no title available |
| `has_first_frame` | boolean | Stored first-frame flag; initialized false |
| `last_frame_age_ms` | integer or null | null if stored last-frame timestamp equals 0; otherwise Int((CACurrentMediaTime() − timestamp) × 1000), truncation toward zero |
| `floater_frame` | object with numeric `x`, `y`, `w`, `h` | Current floater NSWindow.frame in native AppKit coordinates, not source window bounds |

Nil values are retained as JSON null by the optional dictionary/AnyCodable path; pid and last_frame_age_ms are not omitted. There are no `running`, `fps`, `frame_count`, `source_bounds`, `width`, `height`, or error-string fields in this status.

Field producers: window_id `0x100049110–148`; pid `0x10004914c–194`; title `0x100049198–1c8`; has_first_frame `0x1000491cc–210`; last_frame_age_ms `0x100049208–2b0`; floater_frame `0x1000492b4–388`. Timestamp-age computation has finite/Int range traps (`0x1000493ec–3f4`); these are not ordinary expected runtime errors.

#### ArmedStatus (three exact fields)

Dictionary mapper `0x10004a1ec–0x10004a698`, construction `0x10004a2f4–0x10004a378`:

    {"window_id":123,"pid":456,"title":"Window title"}

window_id = key as Int; pid = stored signed Int32 as Int; title = stored nonoptional String. Missing/empty title was stored as `""`, so title is not null. No bool armed flag, observer ID, timestamp, expiry, or preferred size is included.

### 4. `pip_arm`

Entry `0x10007cbc8`; resolver `0x100089414`; armer `0x10004e480`.

#### Parsed arguments / resolution difference

Reads `window_id` via int→UInt32, `title` via str, `pid` via int→Int32, `bundle` via str. No width/height or size is read.

The direct path requires **both** parsed window_id and parsed pid:

    if window_id != nil and pid != nil:
        resolved = (window_id, pid, title ?? "")
    else:
        app = resolveApp(bundle, pid)
        windows = (try? AXBridge.windows(app)) ?? []
        if windows.empty:
            throw internal("App has no windows to arm.")
        selected = same selectWindow rules as pip_open
        resolved = (selected.id, app.processIdentifier, selected.title)

Consequently `pip_arm({window_id:123,bundle:"Example"})` without pid resolves Example and chooses a window; it does **not** guarantee arming window 123. With no pid/bundle, window_id alone reaches shared app resolution and fails. Conversely explicit window_id+pid avoids app-resolution liveness/ownership validation, then the armer checks that ID occurs in that PID's AX windows. Direct path `0x100089444–46c` then `0x1000896f0–71c`; no-ID/no-PID path starts `0x100089470`.

Selection is exact title, contains title, focused, first; no title match error. Empty title becomes nil when passed to the armer (`0x10007cd58–0x10007cf10`); armer stores nil as empty String.

#### Response

    {"armed":{"window_id":123,"pid":456,"title":"Window title"}}

The inner title is the resolved String, including `""`. `armed` is an object, not a Boolean or an array. Construction `0x10007d05c–0x10007d14c` then shared dictionary assembly `0x10007bd44–0x10007bd8c`.

Arming an already armed window ID is a no-op success: armer returns after its locked lookup (`0x10004e4e4–0x10004e524`). It does not update stored PID/title or replace observers. Dispatch still replies using this call's resolved tuple. Therefore a duplicate arm's response metadata can differ from later pip_status.armed metadata; status reports the original registration.

For a fresh entry, armer reads AXWindows for the supplied PID, finds first AX element whose `_AXUIElementGetWindow` ID equals window_id, creates AXObserver, adds AXWindowMiniaturized and AXUIElementDestroyed notifications, adds its source to main run loop, then stores (pid, element, title, observer). `_AXUIElementGetWindow`'s return code is not checked; output starts at zero. A zero ID can therefore behave unusually, rather than being explicitly rejected.

#### Errors and success caveat

- Shared app-resolution error
- `internal`: `App has no windows to arm.` (AX enumeration errors swallowed by resolver)
- `internal`: `No AX window element for windowId=<UInt32> pid=<Int32>` if AXWindows lookup fails, is absent, casts incorrectly, is empty, or has no matching ID. Producer `0x10004e580–0x10004e698`
- `internal`: `AXObserverCreate failed: <AXError integer>` when AXObserverCreate reports nonzero or returns no observer; producer `0x10004e9b0–0x10004ea6c`
- Checked-number traps as above

Both AXObserverAddNotification return codes are ignored (`0x10004e854`, `0x10004e890`). A successful armed response proves the code registered/stored an observer attempt, not that every application actually supports those notifications.

#### Minimize/destroy disposition (newly closed lifecycle detail)

Observer callback `0x100052754–0x100052c64` first requires nonnil context and a nonzero window ID obtained from the callback's element. Other notifications are ignored. Matching AXWindowMiniaturized queues the closure at `0x100052cd8`; AXUIElementDestroyed queues disarm at `0x100052c68`.

On queued minimize:

1. If PIPManager already contains that ID, disarm the entry and return (`0x100052d48–0x100052d7c`)
2. Else read armer's entry; if removed in the meantime, return (`0x100052db0–dd4`)
3. Open using stored ID/PID/nonempty title, no preferred size (`0x100052de4–0x100052e34`)
4. On successful open, release returned status and disarm (`0x100052fa0–0x100052fb4`)
5. On failed open, print `PIP auto-open on minimise failed for wid=<id>: <error>\n` to stderr and retain the armed entry (`0x100052e38–0x100052f9c`)

**Correction to a broader earlier lifecycle summary:** error does not auto-disarm here. The arming is one-shot on successful/existing open, but a failed attempt remains eligible for a later notification. The prior command response is not replaced by a later failure response.

### 5. `pip_disarm`

Entry `0x10007cc90`; armer single disarm `0x10004eabc`; all `0x10004ec88`.

Same argument contract as pip_close: `all` bool default false, otherwise required int→UInt32 `window_id`. With all true, skips window_id parsing and returns:

    {"disarmed":"all"}

Single mode:

    {"disarmed":true,"window_id":123}

False means no entry existed, still success. Missing/unparseable ID produces exactly the same `invalid_arg` message as close:

    missing "window_id" (or pass "all": true)

Disarm removes the dictionary entry under lock, then removes main-run-loop source and both AX notifications; it returns whether an entry existed (`0x10004eabc–0x10004ec14`). Notification-removal return codes are ignored. It does not close a live PiP session.

Caller evidence: all branch `0x10007cc90–0x10007cd1c`; missing branch `0x10007ce74–0x10007cef4`; UInt32 checks `0x10007cfcc–0x10007cfdc`, traps `0x10007d1a0/1a4`; response `0x10007cff0–0x10007d058` sharing single-close tail.

### Exact residual gaps and replication tests

No unresolved parsed argument keys or response field names remain for these five commands. Remaining verification belongs to runtime/system behavior, not guessed schema:

1. Under each macOS/permission state, enumerate native ScreenCaptureKit/AX localized errors; these are not fixed strings in the binary
2. Observe AppKit-enforced frame for extreme/tiny/preferred sizes, display configuration, and cascade positioning; reported `floater_frame` is actual NSWindow.frame
3. Test timing: open before first frame, async capture failure, close while frame callback pending, armed callback queued while user disarms, simultaneous duplicate opens/arms
4. Test framework capability failures: ignored AX add/remove notification errors and whether minimized/offscreen windows remain shareable
5. Shared generic argument helper dynamic casts, nonfinite numeric String input and trapping integer conversions need fixture tests on Swift/Foundation for byte-exact parity; the actual branches are documented, no Alma runtime was invoked

Useful parity fixtures: all=true plus invalid window_id; all="true" plus no ID; nonexistent single close/disarm; direct pip_open without pid; direct pip_arm with/without pid; unmatched title with focused fallback; AX enumeration failure; only one valid dimension; zero/negative dimension; repeated open preserving prior metadata/size; repeated arm echoing new metadata while status retains old; no first frame producing null age; failed minimize auto-open retaining armed entry.

### Evidence excerpt files

- `pip-open-resolution.asm` (`0x100088f74–0x100089410`)
- `pip-arm-resolution.asm` (`0x100089414–0x100089898`; includes a short next-function prefix)
- `pip-session-status.asm` (`0x1000490a4–0x1000493f4`)
- `pip-armed-status.asm` (`0x10004a1ec–0x10004a698`)
- `pip-arm.asm` (`0x10004e480–0x10004eab8`)
- `pip-arm-return.asm` (`0x10007d05c–0x10007d14c`)
- `pip-disarm-missing.asm`, `pip-disarm.asm`, `pip-status-lock.asm`
- `pip-manager-open.asm`, `pip-open-on-main.asm`, `pip-scwindow-lookup.asm`, `pip-session-init.asm`, `pip-manager-close.asm`
- `pip-arm-callback.asm` covers observer routing; minimize body `0x100052cd8–0x10005306c` remains in full `arm64-disasm.txt`

## APPSHOT EXTRA CONTRACTS
证据文件：analysis/native-snapshot/APPSHOT_EXTRA_CONTRACTS.md


Binary: Alma 0.4.164 bundled AlmaComputerUse ARM64 slice. Static disassembly only; the target was never executed. All addresses below are virtual addresses in `analysis/native/arm64.macho`; instruction evidence is in `analysis/native/arm64-disasm.txt` and `analysis/native-snapshot/dispatch.asm`. Swift small-string words were decoded from live MOV/MOVK values rather than inferred from nearby ADRP comments. This document describes native commands; Electron may impose additional defaults or transformations.

### 1. Shared parsing conventions

The request's optional parameter dictionary is accessed through native helpers:

- String helper `0x100006140`: conditional cast to String only. Missing/null/wrong type returns nil; a present empty string remains empty.
- Integer helper `0x100006268`: Int, then Double-to-Int truncation toward zero, then decimal String parsing. Floating nonfinite/out-of-Int-range conversion traps. This means `pid`, `window_id`, and `max_width` are integer-like rather than strict JSON-integer fields.
- No required-string helper is called by the appshot commands below. There is no dispatch-level rejection of unknown parameter keys.

The ordinary daemon response envelope belongs to the shared protocol contract. Shapes below describe the command result, not an additional nested envelope.

### 2. `appshot_monitor_start`

Entry `0x10007ea8c`; main continuation `0x10007ef8c`.

#### Parameters and validation

| Key | Native parse | Default | Validation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hotkey` | optional String | nil | If present and nonempty, must exactly match `DoubleCommand`, `DoubleOption`, or `DoubleShift` |

Missing, wrong-type, null, and empty hotkey are all mapped to optional-enum nil. There is no native default to DoubleCommand. Nonempty unknown/case-mismatched text throws `CUError.invalidArg` with the exact interpolated message:

`Unknown hotkey: <value>. Valid: DoubleCommand, DoubleOption, DoubleShift`

Evidence: parse `0x10007ea8c–0x10007ead8`, error assembly `0x10007eadc–0x10007eb70`, optional conversion `0x100082bd8–0x100082c24`, exact enum string switch `0x10006f2ac–0x10006f304`.

#### Subscription and shared monitor state

1. If a ConnectionContext exists, add a subscription to `appshot.monitor` unless that connection's token list already contains that exact topic
2. Likewise subscribe once to `appshot.update`
3. Tokens are stored as `(topic:String, token:UInt64)` in the connection
4. Callback weakly captures the connection and forwards serialized event lines through ConnectionContext.send(line:)
5. Regardless of whether a connection context exists, call global singleton `BareModifierMonitor.start(hotkey:)`

Subscriptions happen before starting the monitor and are not rolled back when its start result reports failure. Repeated start does not duplicate either topic subscription, but does reconfigure the global monitor, including when the caller changes the hotkey. Thus clients do not get independent per-connection hotkeys.

Evidence: monitor dedup `0x100082c28`, update dedup `0x100082cbc`; subscription/store `0x10007ef8c–0x10007f13c`; weak forwarding `0x100089a6c–0x100089ac0` (update thunk at `0x10008b06c` routes through the same implementation).

#### Result

```json
{"started":true,"hotkey":"DoubleCommand","supported":true,"error":null}
```

Fields are `started:Bool`, `hotkey:String?`, `supported:Bool`, `error:String?`. `hotkey` echoes the parsed requested enum, not a later read of actual tap state. Nil hotkey is represented as optional nil in the result dictionary.

- `supported` is a fresh `AXIsProcessTrusted()!=0` call (`0x10006db04`)
- `start` first checks `AXIsProcessTrusted`; failure returns `(false,"Accessibility permission not granted")`
- It ensures a monitor run-loop thread, with a semaphore wait bounded at 1 second; absent run-loop returns `(false,"Failed to start Appshots monitor thread")`
- If a run-loop exists it schedules `applyHotkeyOnMonitorThread`, wakes the loop and immediately returns `(true,nil)`
- Therefore `started:true` confirms scheduling, not successful CGEventTap creation
- With nil hotkey, the scheduled operation tears down the old tap, sets current hotkey to nil, and returns without installing a new tap or broadcasting ready

Evidence: result assembly `0x10007f174–0x10007f490` and `0x10007f700–0x10007f728`; start `0x10006db78–0x10006dcf0`; run-loop wait `0x10006dea4–0x10006dee0`; apply nil branch `0x10006df78–0x10006dfb8`.

#### Monitor event payloads

All of these use topic `appshot.monitor`:

```json
{"type":"ready","hotkey":"DoubleCommand"}
{"type":"error","message":"CGEvent.tapCreate returned nil — Accessibility not granted to Alma Computer Use, or a higher-priority tap claimed the slot"}
{"type":"down","hotkey":"DoubleCommand","frontmost":{}}
```

- Ready is broadcast after the tap is installed/enabled and current hotkey updated
- Tap failure is asynchronous: it can follow a successful `started:true` command response; the same diagnostic is also written to stderr with `[appshot-monitor] ` prefix
- Down includes `frontmost` only when `Appshot.frontmostWindow(excludeBundleId:"com.yetone.alma")` finds a candidate; absent candidate means the key is omitted, not an empty object
- The down event does not itself invoke `Appshot.capture`; it supplies context for the client to decide what to capture
- The native exclusion for hotkey-triggered lookup is hard-coded to `com.yetone.alma`

Evidence: tap creation/apply `0x10006dfbc–0x10006e47c`; down construction and frontmost attachment `0x10006f758–0x10006fa38`.

#### Low-level hotkey detection

Instruction-proven constraints, useful for compatibility tests:

- CGEventTapCreate is invoked with location 1, placement 0, options 1, event mask `0x1000` (`flagsChanged` event type 12)
- Handler ignores event types other than 12
- DoubleCommand checks device-dependent flag pair `(0x8,0x10)`; DoubleOption `(0x20,0x40)`; DoubleShift `(0x2,0x4)`
- It tracks the prior left/right bits; any bit from `0x7e` outside the selected pair clears the pending tap timestamp
- Both selected bits held can fire; a rising selected-side transition can also fire if the prior pending timestamp is at most 0.35 seconds old
- A last-fire timestamp enforces a 0.5-second cooldown; release during cooldown clears pending tap state
- An old pending timestamp is cleared once it exceeds 0.35 seconds

These points come from `0x10006e874–0x10006edc4`; exact host keyboard hardware behavior remains a runtime validation item. The named Double variants should not be simplified to generic keyDown events.

### 3. `appshot_monitor_stop`

Entry `0x10007ee2c`. No parsed parameters. Result:

```json
{"stopped":true}
```

Important asymmetry:

1. With a ConnectionContext, filter its token list for the exact topic `appshot.monitor`
2. Unsubscribe those tokens and remove those same entries from the connection
3. Leave `appshot.update` tokens installed
4. Query the global subscriber count for `appshot.monitor`
5. Stop the global BareModifierMonitor only when that count is zero
6. Return `stopped:true` regardless of whether another connection keeps the monitor active

Without context it cannot remove anyone's subscription, but still performs the count/conditional stop. The stop method schedules teardown onto the run loop if one exists; the response is not a barrier proving teardown completed.

Evidence: dispatch `0x10007ee2c–0x10007ef80`; exact-topic filter `0x10008989c–0x100089a60`; same-topic remove predicate `0x1000845f4–0x100084864`; native stop `0x10006e4a4–0x10006e59c`.

A faithful client must not assume monitor_stop removes update delivery. Repeated stop is harmless at this layer. Connection-disconnect cleanup is a separate daemon path.

### 4. `appshot_capture`

Entry `0x10007f4d8`; async closure/run bridge `0x10007f8f0–0x10007f984`; native capture `0x100070d9c` and continuations.

#### Parameters

| Key | Parse and native type | Default / behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `bundle` | optional String | nil; passed to AXBridge.resolveApp |
| `pid` | integer-like → optional Int32 | nil; values outside `[-2147483648,2147483647]` trap rather than structured validation error |
| `window_id` | integer-like → optional UInt32 | nil; negative or >4294967295 values trap rather than structured validation error |
| `out_dir` | optional String | `NSTemporaryDirectory().appending("alma-appshots")` |
| `max_width` | optional integer-like Int | nil; forwarded to capture helper without dispatch clamp |

`max_width` is confirmed from live x20 small-string first word `max_widt` plus final `h`, not an ADRP comment. The dispatch does not parse `output_path`, `format`, `include_ax`, `depth`, `quality`, or `interactive_only` for this command. Empty string out_dir is retained; only nil triggers the default.

Evidence: bundle/pid `0x10007f4d8–0x10007f51c`; Int32 check and window_id `0x10007f7d0–0x10007f85c`; out_dir/default `0x10007f85c–0x10007f8ec`; max_width `0x10007f8fc–0x10007f918`.

#### Result schema

```json
{
  "imagePath":"/…/appshot-<epochMilliseconds>-<windowId>.jpg",
  "width":1280,
  "height":800,
  "scaleFactor":2.0,
  "axText":"",
  "app":{
    "bundleIdentifier":"com.example.app",
    "name":"Example",
    "pid":123,
    "windowId":456,
    "windowTitle":"Example window",
    "windowFrame":{"x":0,"y":25,"width":1280,"height":800},
    "iconDataURL":"data:image/png;base64,…",
    "scaleFactor":2.0
  }
}
```

The dimensions above are illustrative, not defaults. Exact top-level fields and types:

- `imagePath:String`: path returned by Capture.Result
- `width:Int`, `height:Int`: actual capture-result dimensions
- `scaleFactor:Double`: main-screen backingScaleFactor, fallback 2.0
- `axText:String`: initially **always empty**, including when Accessibility is available
- `app`: FrontmostWindow dictionary described below

The command returns no native extra `ok`, `found`, image base64, or image bytes in this result dictionary. `CaptureResult.toDict` is `0x100070bac–0x100070d54`; dictionary contains exactly six entries.

#### Capture sequence and errors

1. Resolve an application using `AXBridge.resolveApp(bundle:pid:)`; normal resolver errors propagate
2. If explicit window_id is absent, choose `WindowList.topWindow(for:resolvedApp.pid)`
3. If that is absent, throw CUError.internalError with `Target app has no on-screen window to appshot`
4. Create out_dir with intermediate directories enabled; directory-creation error is caught/discarded, so a later image-write error can become the externally visible failure
5. Capture a timestamp `Date.timeIntervalSince1970`, multiply by 1000, truncate to Int, and form `appshot-<milliseconds>-<windowId>.jpg` with NSString.appendingPathComponent
6. Try synchronous `Capture.captureWindowFast(windowId:outputPath:maxWidth:)`
7. If fast capture returns nil, await `Capture.captureWindow(...)`; its thrown errors propagate
8. Look up matching WindowList entry for title/frame metadata when available, and encode the resolved application's icon
9. If `AXBridge.isTrusted(prompt:false)` is true, launch background AX work; do not wait for its text before constructing the response
10. Return capture result with empty axText

Explicit window_id is passed directly into the capture path after resolving the app; the shown dispatch/capture preamble does not validate that this window belongs to the resolved pid. Metadata lookup can therefore fail to find a matching window for a successful capture. Do not assert a guaranteed pid/window consistency check.

Both top-level and app.scaleFactor are read from NSScreen.main.backingScaleFactor, each with fallback 2.0; these are not calculated as outputWidth/windowFrame.width and are not necessarily the target display's scale. Name fallback is `Unknown` when localizedName is unavailable. Evidence: capture selection/error `0x100070ee4–0x100071068`; mkdir/path `0x10007107c–0x10007112c` and `0x10007113c–0x1000712c8`; fast/fallback `0x1000712cc–0x1000713e4`; trust/background `0x100071474–0x100071684`; response construction/scales `0x10007168c–0x10007180c`.

#### Background AX update

When trusted, a background operation creates a separate worker queue named `alma-appshot-ax-worker-<timestamp>` and waits on its semaphore for up to 3 seconds. The worker invokes:

`AXBridge.snapshot(bundle:nil, pid:<resolved pid>, windowId:<captured window>, depth:12, interactiveOnly:false, store:<daemon shared ElementStore>, wakeupSeconds:0.2)`

Snapshot exceptions are swallowed and yield empty AX text. The worker converts snapshot data to text, writes it into a shared string box and signals the semaphore. On timeout the waiting operation writes `[appshot] AX snapshot timed out (pid=<pid>)\n` to stderr, then still sends one update with the box's current text. The worker is not cancelled by timeout; there is no second broadcast in the inspected snapshot-worker closure if it finishes later.

Event:

```json
{"evt":true,"topic":"appshot.update","payload":{"type":"axText","imagePath":"/…/appshot-….jpg","axText":"…"}}
```

`imagePath` is the association key; no command request id is included in this event. No trust means no background worker/update from this path. A capture does not subscribe the caller by itself: monitor_start or some other existing subscription must already have subscribed it to appshot.update to receive this event. All subscribers to that topic receive the update. Scheduling gives no guaranteed ordering between the ordinary response and a sufficiently fast update event.

Evidence: background closure `0x100071f60–0x100072754`, 3-second wait `0x100072488–0x1000724e8`, payload `0x1000725cc–0x100072708`, snapshot call `0x1000731f4–0x100073228`, catch `0x10007322c–0x100073244`, store/signal `0x1000737bc–0x1000737d8`.

#### AX text rendering details

The background worker's text renderer is distinct from the main snapshot JSON serializer:

1. If snapshot.windowTitle is present and nonempty, begin with `# <windowTitle>` and one empty line
2. For each serialized element, create parts beginning with its nonempty `role`, then its nonempty `title`
3. If its `value` is present, nonempty, and different from `title`, append value and skip description
4. Otherwise append nonempty `description` when available
5. If parts is nonempty, append `- ` plus parts joined by the literal separator ` · ` (space, middle dot, space)
6. Join all lines by LF, without an explicitly appended terminal LF

No ref IDs, coordinates, roleDescription, action list, or tree indentation are emitted by this renderer. A nonempty value suppresses description only when it differs from title. Evidence: title/header `0x10007324c–0x100073318`; role/title/value/description collection `0x100073340–0x10007356c`; element separator and prefix `0x1000735b4–0x1000735e0`; final LF join `0x100073788–0x1000737a0`. SerializedElement getter offsets cross-check the loaded fields: role +0x10, title +0x40, value +0x50, description +0x60.

### 5. FrontmostWindow dictionary and `appshot_frontmost`

The shared `FrontmostWindow.toDict` (`0x10006fc1c–0x100070048`) produces eight entries:

| Field | Type |
|---|---|
| `bundleIdentifier` | String? |
| `name` | String |
| `pid` | Int from signed Int32 |
| `windowId` | Int from UInt32? |
| `windowTitle` | String? |
| `windowFrame` | optional object `{x,y,width,height}`, all CGFloat numeric values |
| `iconDataURL` | String? |
| `scaleFactor` | Double |

Optional values are nil in the Swift `[String:Any?]` dictionary; normal response encoding determines JSON-null representation. They must not be invented when unavailable. The appshot monitor's absent `frontmost` outer key is omitted, independently of these inner optional values.

Additional directly inspected dispatch:

- `appshot_frontmost` entry `0x10007f31c`
- Parameter `exclude_bundle_id`: optional String, default nil
- Calls Appshot.frontmostWindow(excludeBundleId:)
- No candidate: `{found:false}`
- Candidate: `{found:true, ...FrontmostWindow.toDict()}` (fields merged at result top level, not under an `app` or `frontmost` wrapper)

Evidence: parse and no-candidate branch `0x10007f328–0x10007f408`; merge `0x10007f520–0x10007f6fc`. Its helper scans CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo and applies native filtering; exact native frontmost-selection filters are outside this supplement's exhaustive claims.

### 6. Appshot event wire format

`AppshotEvents.broadcast(topic:payload:)`, `0x10006bd54–0x10006c134`, serializes:

```json
{"evt":true,"topic":"appshot.monitor","payload":{"type":"ready","hotkey":"DoubleCommand"}}
```

- Exactly outer keys `evt`, `topic`, `payload`; event has no request id/result envelope
- JSONSerialization options integer 8; one LF byte is appended
- JSON serialization failure is caught and discarded, with no emitted event
- It snapshots callbacks for the topic while locked, unlocks, and invokes them
- Connection callback uses a weak reference and simply sends the serialized line if the connection is still alive

Consumers should identify events by evt/topic and route them independently of id-matched ordinary command responses.

### 7. `shutdown`

Entry `0x10007e55c`; no parameters read. Enqueues shutdown onto DispatchQueue.main, then constructs result:

```json
{"shutting_down":true}
```

The scheduled closure at `0x100089ad8`:

1. Checks daemon's shutdown flag at object offset 0x2c; returns if already set
2. Sets that flag
3. Writes `[alma-cu] shutting down\n` to stderr
4. Calls `AXBridge.restoreAllEnhancedUI()`
5. Closes daemon listen/socket fd and stores -1
6. Unlinks daemon socket path
7. Calls exit(0)

The ordinary response is constructed immediately after scheduling, so a strict flush-before-exit guarantee is not established by this static branch. No graceful waiting for outstanding captures/AX workers is visible here. Evidence: scheduling/result `0x10007e55c–0x10007e698`; closure `0x100089ad8–0x100089c08`.

### 8. Focused compatibility tests (not executed)

1. Start with absent/null/empty/incorrect-type hotkey: subscriptions installed, nil requested hotkey; existing global tap is torn down after scheduling
2. Start with lower-case `doublecommand`: exact invalidArg, no subscriptions added because validation precedes setup
3. Start with trusted AX but tap installation failing: first started=true then asynchronous monitor error
4. Two clients start different hotkeys: latest scheduled configuration applies globally; each receives shared monitor events
5. Stop one of two monitor clients: stopped=true while global monitor remains active
6. Stop only monitor client: monitor token removed, update token remains; later capture updates can still arrive
7. Capture with missing app selectors, explicit pid, bundle and conflicting pid: use resolver's native precedence/error rules
8. Capture pid/window_id boundary and noninteger numeric cases: reproduce Int conversion and narrowing-trap behavior or explicitly document safer divergence
9. Capture with missing out_dir vs empty out_dir; verify generated jpg basename and later failure behavior when mkdir fails
10. Capture without AX permission: nonempty image result plus axText="", no AX update
11. Capture with AX success/failure/timeout: response axText="" in each case; one update when trusted; association through imagePath
12. Capture on non-main display or downscaled image: metadata scaleFactor remains main-screen backing scale
13. appshot_frontmost no candidate yields only found=false; found candidate is flattened into result
14. shutdown repeated or concurrent with capture: flag idempotence and socket teardown; response arrival requires runtime validation

## MCP CONTROL EXTRA CONTRACTS
证据文件：analysis/native-snapshot/MCP_CONTROL_EXTRA_CONTRACTS.md


Static arm64 analysis, Alma0.4.164 AlmaComputerUse; all VAs unslid. Target never executed. Scope: seven native socket control commands, their input defaults/errors/data shapes, status/whitelist records, and directly called start/stop/add/list mechanics. **This is not a complete native MCP-over-HTTP/OAuth implementation contract.** Those request routes, wire schemas, peer-signing authorization, grant/code lifecycle and native tool registry must be specified separately.

Use shared native str/int/double/bool reader contracts in REMOTE_LENS_EXTRA_CONTRACTS.md. Numeric-looking strings and Double→Int truncation can reach these handlers; narrow conversions are not uniformly errors.

### 1. Complete dispatcher shell table

| cmd | Inputs parsed | Success data | Core call / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| mcp_start | port:int?; token_path:str? | Status object in§2 | MCPServer.start0x1000546fc, called0x10007ce54 |
| mcp_stop | none | {stopped:true} | MCPServer.stop0x100055818 at0x10007cf8c; shared dictionary tail0x10007ef18–ef60 |
| mcp_status | none | Status object in§2 | MCPServer.statusSnapshot0x100054f7c at0x10007d18c |
| mcp_allow | team_id:str?; bundle_id:str?; pid:int?; ttl_seconds:double? | {allowed:true,team_id:<String or null>,bundle_id:<String or null>,pid:<Int32 or null>,ttl_seconds:<Double or null>} | MCPServer.addWhitelist0x10006348c at0x10007d4e8; response0x10007d514–d570,d630–d674,d83c–d880,d9ec–da40,dc04–dc1c |
| mcp_deny_all | none | {cleared:true} | MCPServer.clearWhitelist0x100063714 at0x10007d2bc; response literal0x10007d2f8–d30c→e694/ef58 |
| mcp_whitelist | none | {entries:[<WhitelistEntry>,...]} | MCPServer.whitelistList0x100063770 at0x10007d464; wrapper0x10007d40c–d4ac |
| mcp_arm_peer | pid:int required; ttl_seconds:double?=120 | {armed:true,pid:<input narrowed Int32>,ttl_seconds:<effective Double>} | addWhitelist at0x10007d788; response0x10007d794–d838 |

All shapes above are the `data` inside the standard NDJSON response envelope. Extra keys are ignored by these dispatcher branches. `mcp_allow` uses **bundle_id**, unlike app-target commands' **bundle**. `mcp_start` uses **token_path**, not token_file or tokenFilePath.

#### mcp_start argument handling

- `port` read0x10007cdec. Values representable as UInt16 (0…65535) are passed as present. Missing/unconvertible values **and parsed integers outside UInt16 range** become nil at0x10007ce20–ce34. This is a silent fallback, not a UInt16 trap or invalidArg. The underlying NWEndpoint.Port(rawValue:) optional is also coalesced to `.any` when absent (0x100054b74–c4c). No fixed default TCP port is encoded here.
- `token_path` optional String read0x10007ce10; nil selects a default token-file path in start. Empty string is present and is forwarded rather than rejected by requireStr.
- If listener already exists (self+0x18), start immediately returns current status0x10005486c–487c. New port/token_path arguments are then ignored; it does not rebind or rotate the token on this already-running path.
- When stopped, start assigns daemon reference, generates a fresh bearer token and chooses token file: explicit token_path if non-nil; otherwise `(environment["HOME"] ?? NSHomeDirectory()) + path component ".alma/cu/mcp-token"` (0x1000548c8–4a44; literal0x100094860). It writes token via0x100067320 before listener construction. The token value is not returned in status.
- Token write creates parent directories (0x10006745c), writes UTF-8 Data (0x1000674ec), then calls chmod0600 (w1=0x180 at0x100067568). The chmod return is not tested in this function. Filesystem/Network framework thrown errors propagate through the ordinary processLine error conversion; no success should be fabricated on write/bind failure.
- Starts NWListener and waits for boundPort!=0, with a two-second deadline and0.01-second polling sleeps (0x100054dd8–4e70; Double constant0x3f847ae147ae147b). On timeout it cancels/clears listener and throws internalError **`MCP listener failed to bind within 2s`** (factory0x100054f0c, text0x100093650).
- Status advertises `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/mcp`; exact HTTP binding/auth behavior remains separate from this control schema.

#### mcp_allow validation and expiry

- Reads team_id0x10007d214, bundle_id0x10007d230, pid0x10007d24c, ttl_seconds0x10007d350.
- Requires at least one of the three identity optionals to be present. If all nil: invalidArg **`need at least one of team_id / bundle_id / pid`** at0x10007d390 (text0x100094d60). Optional Strings are not trimmed or required nonempty here; an empty string passes presence validation.
- A present pid narrows to Int32; outside[-2147483648,2147483647] hits trap0x10007d4b0/4b4. Within range, no positive-PID check is present at this dispatcher boundary.
- Missing/unconvertible ttl_seconds is forwarded as nil. addWhitelist then stores no expiry (0x100063580–635bc). A supplied TTL becomes `Date.now + ttl_seconds` (0x100063598–635a4), with no positivity clamp in this function. Zero/negative values therefore construct current/past expiration dates. NaN/infinity outcomes were not run.
- addWhitelist appends a new entry under lock (0x10006360c–63688); no deduplication/replacement occurs in this method. Multiple identical requests can produce duplicate entries. Response echoes optional identities and optional original TTL, not an expiry timestamp.
- Matching rules across team_id/bundle_id/pid and the moment expiry is enforced are deliberately not inferred from add/list alone.

#### mcp_arm_peer differences

- pid read0x10007d5c4. If absent/unconvertible, invalidArg **missing \`pid\`** where the characters around pid are ASCII backticks (literal assembly0x10007d5d4–d5f0; factory0x10007d5f8). This differs from requireStr's quoted-key error.
- Optional ttl_seconds read0x10007d720; nil selects Double120.0, bit pattern0x405e000000000000 (0x10007d724–d738). Supplied zero/negative TTL is retained.
- PID narrowing traps at0x10007d9e4/e8 when outside Int32; otherwise no positive-PID check in this branch.
- Calls addWhitelist with **nil teamId, nil bundleId, supplied PID, effective TTL** at0x10007d76c–d788. The dispatcher does not infer PID from the socket ConnectionContext despite the command's name, and it does not return verified signing identity.

#### mcp_deny_all and stop scope

- clearWhitelist0x100063714 only replaces the whitelist array with empty under lock (0x10006372c–6375c). Do not describe this as revoking every already-issued OAuth/access grant; those are separate maps and are not cleared by this method.
- stop synchronously cancels tracked NWConnections on its queue (closure0x10006786c–67968), cancels listener, assigns listener=nil and boundPort=0 (0x100055900–55930), then returns {stopped:true}. No token-file unlink is present in this stop function. The inspected code does not clear oauth_client_id/token_path or the whitelist/grant/code maps here; a later status can retain those fields/counts despite running=false.

### 2. Exact Status object

statusSnapshot0x100054f7c–55428 constructs ten fields. Its count snapshots are taken under the server lock; listener/port/string fields are then read separately. Do not assume the entire returned object is one atomic transaction.

| Field | Native data value | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| running | Bool: listener object != nil |0x100055054–5088 |
| port | integer UInt16 boundPort,0 when stopped/unbound |0x10005508c–50ac |
| url | String `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/mcp` if port!=0, otherwise null |0x1000550b0–517c |
| oauth_base | String `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` if port!=0, otherwise null |0x100055180–521c |
| oauth_client_id | Stored String if nonempty, otherwise null |0x100055220–5280 |
| token_path | Stored token-file String if nonempty, otherwise null |0x100055284–52d4 |
| tool_count | Native MCPServer static tools array count |0x1000552d8–5310; initializer0x10005540c→0x10005db18 |
| active_grants | Stored grant dictionary count |reads0x100054fc8–4fd0, emits0x100055314–5348 |
| pending_codes | Stored pending-code dictionary count |reads0x100054fe8–4ff0, emits0x10005534c–5378 |
| whitelist_size | Stored whitelist array count |reads0x100055008–5010, emits0x10005537c–53ac |

Status does not include bearer token content, connected-peer count, complete entries, or verification of current app permissions. The count getters themselves do not filter expired entries in this function. The field name active_grants is not by itself proof that every counted record is unexpired. `tool_count` is for the **native** MCPServer registry; do not substitute23 from the separate JavaScript MCP adapter.

### 3. WhitelistEntry output record

mcp_whitelist returns {entries: [...]} with each entry:

    { "team_id": <String or null>, "bundle_id": <String or null>,
      "pid": <Int32 or null>, "expires_at": <Unix-epoch seconds Double or null> }

Mapping closure0x10006391c–63d54 proves keys and types:

- team_id0x1000639f0–63ab4
- bundle_id0x100063ab4–63b58
- pid0x100063b58–63bec
- expires_at0x100063bec–63cf0; Date.timeIntervalSince1970 at0x100063cbc

Absent values are explicitly boxed as NSNull in this non-optional Any dictionary. The function iterates the stored array in order, mapping every entry under lock0x1000637cc–638f0. There is no sort, filtering or expiry cleanup in whitelistList itself. As elsewhere, another server operation could mutate the array before this call.

### 4. Minimum static conformance cases

1. mcp_start with no port, port0, negative port,65535,65536; invalid-range inputs take `.any` path rather than trapping
2. While running, repeated mcp_start with different token_path/port returns existing status without applying them
3. token_path nil versus empty; read/write errors propagate; do not invent a success status
4. mcp_allow with no identity -> exact error; empty team_id -> admitted presence check
5. mcp_allow omitted TTL -> null response TTL and expires_at:null; positive TTL -> epoch seconds; zero/negative TTL retained
6. Duplicate mcp_allow calls append duplicate entries and increase raw whitelist_size
7. mcp_arm_peer omitted TTL ->120; missing pid -> backtick-quoted exact error; present pid+negative TTL retained
8. mcp_deny_all empties entries but is not a blanket OAuth grant revocation
9. mcp_stop returns stopped:true; status runningfalse/port0/urlnull/oauth_basenull; file path/client ID and maps may remain
10. Whitelist record null fields must serialize as JSON null, not omitted keys, strings or empty dictionaries

These are specified fixtures, not executed product test results.

### 5. Explicit unresolved native-server scope

The seven socket-control shells and their ordinary field/result/error contracts above are statically covered. Full native MCP server parity still requires: native tool registration schemas, JSON-RPC result/error behavior, HTTP request framing/body limits/connection semantics, OAuth metadata/authorize/token routes, PKCE and redirect validation, signing/peer identity extraction, whitelist AND/OR match semantics and expiry enforcement, access-grant/pending-code cleanup and revocation, and protocol/session negotiation. None of those is interchangeable with the JavaScript MCP23 adapter or host HTTP API. A standalone implementation may defer this server deliberately, but a claim of complete Alma parity may not silently omit it.

## PARITY SURFACE
证据文件：analysis/native-snapshot/PARITY_SURFACE.md


Evidence cross-check: analysis/host/host-contract-report.md lines61–69 identifies native helper forwarding; analysis/mirror-ui/behavioral-contract.md identifies actual Electron mirror pump shot/shot_display; remote_input is exposed through host/remote WebSocket plumbing, not the view-only mirror UI; analysis/mcp/REPORT.zh-CN.md maps23JS tools to13 distinct native commands. Mere forwarding is distinguished from an observed default caller. No45-command whole-system implementation-ready claim is made.

Categories:

- CORE-MCP: direct current JS MCP adapter call path
- CORE-HOST: host helper forwarding is recovered, but a specific normal UI invocation may be optional/untraced
- CORE-MIRROR: actual default Electron mirror frame path traced; mirror is view-only
- CORE-APPSHOT: actual Appshot native workflow/host path traced
- OPTIONAL-NATIVE: native/administrative surface exists, no default JS/Electron caller established in inspected reports

| Command | Category | Contract source / implementation boundary |
|---|---|---|
| ping | CORE-HOST | UTILITY_COMMANDS; daemon health/connection |
| permissions | CORE-MCP | UTILITY_COMMANDS |
| grant | CORE-MCP | UTILITY_COMMANDS |
| apps | CORE-HOST | UTILITY_COMMANDS; distinct regular-GUI-app listing |
| list_apps | CORE-MCP | APP_USAGE_CONTRACT + APP_INTENTS_METADATA; exact history source/merge/sort and emitted registry metadata |
| windows | CORE-HOST | UTILITY_COMMANDS / FINDINGS10 |
| snap | CORE-HOST | FINDINGS2–4; not wrapper post-action path |
| launch_app | CORE-MCP | MCP/native dispatch/native launch; host contract |
| open_url | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | UTILITY_COMMANDS shell+quiet opener; no JS23 registration |
| intent | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | UTILITY_COMMANDS execution shell; metadata is core via list_apps (APP_INTENTS_METADATA), URL-builder execution templates deferred |
| get_app_state | CORE-MCP | FINDINGS2–11; native diff report |
| click | CORE-MCP | Native event/ref algorithms + WIRE_SCHEMA targeting |
| type | CORE-HOST | Native ref text algorithms |
| type_text | CORE-MCP | Native text algorithms + MCP report |
| press | CORE-HOST | Native ref key algorithms |
| press_key | CORE-MCP | Native key algorithms + MCP report |
| perform_secondary_action | CORE-MCP | Native event/ref algorithms |
| drag | CORE-MCP | Native event algorithms |
| set_value | CORE-MCP | Native ref algorithms |
| select_text | CORE-MCP | Native ref algorithms |
| scroll | CORE-MCP | Native event/ref algorithms |
| lens | CORE-HOST | REMOTE_LENS_EXTRA_CONTRACTS; native overlay is separate from Electron PiP |
| pip_open | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | PIP_EXTRA_CONTRACTS; not traced as Electron mirror implementation |
| pip_close | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | PIP_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| pip_status | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | PIP_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| pip_arm | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | PIP_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| pip_disarm | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | PIP_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| mcp_start | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Native standalone MCP service control shell; HTTP/OAuth/RPC parity deferred |
| mcp_stop | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Same |
| mcp_status | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Same |
| mcp_allow | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Same; security policy is separate from user-facing confirmations |
| mcp_deny_all | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Same |
| mcp_whitelist | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Same |
| mcp_arm_peer | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Same |
| raise | CORE-HOST | UTILITY_COMMANDS; host middleware exists, runtime AXRaise behavior unverified |
| shot | CORE-MIRROR | UTILITY_COMMANDS / FINDINGS capture / mirror reports |
| shot_display | CORE-MIRROR | UTILITY_COMMANDS; display fallback captures system cursor pixels |
| remote_input | CORE-HOST | Host/remote WebSocket input route; not default view-only mirror input. REMOTE_LENS_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| stealth_stats | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Dispatcher diagnostic path; exact record contract requires native diagnostic supplement |
| turn_ended | OPTIONAL-NATIVE | Dispatcher releaseAll gate; verify host call before promoting to CORE |
| shutdown | CORE-HOST | APPSHOT_EXTRA_CONTRACTS shutdown + host lifecycle |
| appshot_monitor_start | CORE-APPSHOT | APPSHOT_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| appshot_monitor_stop | CORE-APPSHOT | APPSHOT_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| appshot_frontmost | CORE-APPSHOT | APPSHOT_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |
| appshot_capture | CORE-APPSHOT | APPSHOT_EXTRA_CONTRACTS |

The native standalone MCPServer is not interchangeable with the product's traced JS stdio MCP bridge. Its OAuth grants/peer-whitelist HTTP transport is a distinct optional product capability. Control-shell wire schemas do not establish OAuth or full-RPC interoperability. Keep that backend disabled/unsupported in a core clone until implemented; do not expose an insecure permissive substitute.

Core static protocol/algorithm reconstruction is not runtime verification: TCC, private API behavior, focus races, AX fidelity, ScreenCaptureKit scaling and app launch remain macOS acceptance-test gates. PIP/Appshot/native-extra supplement documents likewise separate static contracts from live OS proof.

## 共用错误映射
- prefix：/api/computer-use

- helper_error_mapping：{"http400_codes":["ax_not_granted","sc_not_granted","invalid_arg","app_not_found","element_not_found","ref_stale","action_unsupported","unsupported_platform"],"other_typed_errors_http":500,"typed_error_body":{"error":"message","code":"code","hint":"hint when defined"},"untyped_errors_http":500,"untyped_error_body":{"error":"exception message or computer-use failed","code":"internal"}}

Other listed POST actions registered later do run it. It can run for other methods on listed paths before eventual404; it is not a POST-only middleware.
- not_authorization：Activity notification is not an approval check
